  17: /// @title OmrV4TwapOracle - a geometric TWAP for the canonical native ETH/OMR Uniswap v4 pool.
  18: ///
  19: /// @notice Feeds the unchanged `IOmrOracle` surface consumed by OmertaBond. Uniswap v4's
  20: ///         PoolManager exposes current slot0 state but does not maintain the v2/v3 historical
  21: ///         observation series the bond mint wall needs. The canonical OmertaHook therefore keeps a
  22: ///         minimal tick-time accumulator on every successful swap; this contract closes bounded
  23: ///         windows over that cumulative and converts the arithmetic mean tick into OMR per ETH.
  24: ///
  25: ///         MISSED POKES DO NOT LOSE THE PRICE PATH. Keeper calls only decide when a completed window
  26: ///         is published. Every swap updates the source accumulator, and quiet time is brought forward
  27: ///         counterfactually at read time. A keeper outage makes the oracle stale; it cannot make a
  28: ///         spot price masquerade as a TWAP.
  29: ///
  30: ///         DEPLOYMENT DOES NOT REQUIRE AN OPEN POOL. The immutable source and pool identity may be
  31: ///         pinned before genesis migration. Until an initialized observation seeds a baseline,
  32: ///         consult returns no quote. The first seed also returns no quote: only a full subsequent
  33: ///         PERIOD of initialized pool history can produce the first reading.
  34: ///
  35: ///         BOTH SIDES OF THE WINDOW ARE BOUNDED. A call before `PERIOD` reverts (or no-ops when it
  36: ///         arrives through the hook's observer seam). A call after `PERIOD * MAX_WINDOW_MULT`
  37: ///         discards the obsolete interval, clears the public reading, and re-baselines. Recovery is
  38: ///         one honest window. This is the same fail-closed policy as OmrTwapOracle's audited V2 path.
  39: ///
  40: ///         The result is a geometric time-weighted mean, the standard tick-cumulative construction:
  41: ///         ticks are logarithms of price, so averaging tick and converting once yields geometric
  42: ///         rather than arithmetic mean price. OMR and native ETH both use 18 decimals, making the
  43: ///         v4 currency1/currency0 raw-unit ratio exactly OMR-wei per ETH-wei.
  44: contract OmrV4TwapOracle is IOmrOracle, IOmrHookObserver {
  45:     using PoolIdLibrary for PoolKey;
  46: 
  47:     uint32 public immutable PERIOD;
  48:     uint32 public constant MIN_PERIOD = 10 minutes;
  49:     uint32 public constant MAX_WINDOW_MULT = 4;
  50: 
  51:     IOmrV4ObservationSource public immutable source;
  52:     IPoolManager public immutable poolManager;
  53:     address public immutable omr;
  54:     uint24 public immutable fee;
  55:     int24 public immutable tickSpacing;
  56:     PoolId public immutable poolId;
  57: 
  58:     // These values pack into one slot. The cumulative and timestamp deliberately follow the
  59:     // v3 int56/uint32 wrapping convention; their differences remain correct across timestamp wrap.
  60:     int56 public tickCumulativeLast;
  61:     uint32 public blockTimestampLast;
  62:     int24 public arithmeticMeanTick;
  63:     // Timestamp zero is valid at chain genesis and uint32 wrap, so it cannot be an initialization flag.
  64:     bool public baselineInitialized;
  65: 
  66:     uint256 public priceAverage;
  67:     uint256 public lastUpdate;
  68: 
  69:     event Updated(int24 arithmeticMeanTick, uint256 omrPerEth, uint32 timeElapsed);
  70:     event Rebaselined(uint32 discardedWindow);
  71:     event Invalidated(int24 arithmeticMeanTick);
  72:     event BaselineInitialized(int56 tickCumulative, uint32 blockTimestamp);
  73: 
  74:     error PeriodTooShort();
  75:     error PeriodNotElapsed(uint32 elapsed, uint32 required);
  76:     error ZeroAddress();
  77:     error ContractRequired(address target);
  78:     error UnsupportedObservationSource();
  79:     error UnsupportedTokenDecimals(address token, uint8 decimals);
  80:     error PoolNotInitialized();
  81:     error WrongPool();
  82:     error NotObservationSource();
  83: 
  84:     /// @param source_ the exact OmertaHook used by the canonical v4 pool
  85:     /// @param omr_ OMR; native ETH is fixed as currency0 and OMR as currency1
  86:     /// @param fee_ the canonical pool fee committed by the launch configuration
  87:     /// @param tickSpacing_ the canonical pool tick spacing committed by the launch configuration
  88:     /// @param period_ minimum number of chain seconds in one published observation window
  89:     constructor(IOmrV4ObservationSource source_, address omr_, uint24 fee_, int24 tickSpacing_, uint32 period_) {
  90:         if (address(source_) == address(0) || omr_ == address(0)) revert ZeroAddress();
  91:         if (address(source_).code.length == 0) revert ContractRequired(address(source_));
  92:         if (omr_.code.length == 0) revert ContractRequired(omr_);
  93:         if (period_ < MIN_PERIOD) revert PeriodTooShort();
  94:         if (!IERC165(address(source_)).supportsInterface(type(IOmrV4ObservationSource).interfaceId)) {
  95:             revert UnsupportedObservationSource();
  96:         }
  97: 
  98:         IPoolManager poolManager_ = source_.poolManager();
  99:         if (address(poolManager_) == address(0)) revert ZeroAddress();
 100:         if (address(poolManager_).code.length == 0) revert ContractRequired(address(poolManager_));
 101: 
 102:         uint8 omrDecimals = IERC20Metadata(omr_).decimals();
 103:         if (omrDecimals != 18) revert UnsupportedTokenDecimals(omr_, omrDecimals);
 104: 
 105:         PoolKey memory key = PoolKey({
 106:             currency0: Currency.wrap(address(0)),
 107:             currency1: Currency.wrap(omr_),
 108:             fee: fee_,
 109:             tickSpacing: tickSpacing_,
 110:             hooks: IHooks(address(source_))
 111:         });
 112:         PoolId poolId_ = key.toId();
 113:         (int56 cumulative, uint32 timestamp, bool initialized) = source_.currentTickCumulative(poolId_);
 114: 
 115:         source = source_;
 116:         poolManager = poolManager_;
 117:         omr = omr_;
 118:         fee = fee_;
 119:         tickSpacing = tickSpacing_;
 120:         poolId = poolId_;
 121:         PERIOD = period_;
 122:         if (initialized) _setBaseline(cumulative, timestamp);
 123:     }
 124: 
 125:     /// @notice Permissionless keeper entry point. The first initialized call seeds a baseline;
 126:     ///         nobody can close a window before a full PERIOD after that baseline.
 127:     function update() external {
 128:         _update(true);
 129:     }
 130: 
 131:     /// @inheritdoc IOmrHookObserver
 132:     /// @dev Called through OmertaHook.pokeObserver after PoolManager settlement. Frequent swap
 133:     ///      notifications simply no-op until a full window is available, avoiding a revert on every
 134:     ///      trade while preserving the strict behavior of the direct keeper entry point.
 135:     function observe(PoolKey calldata key) external {
 136:         if (msg.sender != address(source)) revert NotObservationSource();
 137:         if (PoolId.unwrap(key.toId()) != PoolId.unwrap(poolId)) revert WrongPool();
 138:         _update(false);
 139:     }
 140: 
 141:     /// @inheritdoc IOmrOracle
 142:     function consult() external view returns (uint256 omrPerEth, uint256 updatedAt) {
 143:         return (priceAverage, lastUpdate);
 144:     }
 145: 
 146:     function _update(bool revertIfEarly) private {
 147:         (int56 cumulative, uint32 timestamp, bool initialized) = source.currentTickCumulative(poolId);
 148:         if (!initialized) {
 149:             if (revertIfEarly) revert PoolNotInitialized();
 150:             return;
 151:         }
 152: 
 153:         if (!baselineInitialized) {
 154:             _setBaseline(cumulative, timestamp);
 155:             return;
 156:         }
 157: 
 158:         uint32 timeElapsed;
 159:         unchecked {
 160:             timeElapsed = timestamp - blockTimestampLast;
 161:         }
 162:         if (timeElapsed < PERIOD) {
 163:             if (revertIfEarly) revert PeriodNotElapsed(timeElapsed, PERIOD);
 164:             return;
 165:         }
 166: 
 167:         if (timeElapsed > PERIOD * MAX_WINDOW_MULT) {
 168:             _setBaseline(cumulative, timestamp);
 169:             arithmeticMeanTick = 0;
 170:             priceAverage = 0;
 171:             lastUpdate = 0;
 172:             emit Rebaselined(timeElapsed);
 173:             return;
 174:         }
 175: 
 176:         int56 cumulativeDelta;
 177:         unchecked {
 178:             cumulativeDelta = cumulative - tickCumulativeLast;
 179:         }
 180:         int56 denominator = int56(uint56(timeElapsed));
 181:         int56 meanTick = cumulativeDelta / denominator;
 182:         // Solidity rounds signed division toward zero. Tick TWAPs conventionally round toward
 183:         // negative infinity so a negative fractional tick does not become a more favorable price.
 184:         if (cumulativeDelta < 0 && (cumulativeDelta % denominator != 0)) meanTick--;
 185: 
 186:         int24 meanTick24 = int24(meanTick);
 187:         uint256 price = _omrPerEthAtTick(meanTick24);
 188:         _setBaseline(cumulative, timestamp);
 189:         arithmeticMeanTick = meanTick24;
 190:         if (price == 0) {
 191:             // At an extreme negative tick the 18-decimal quote genuinely rounds below one OMR wei
 192:             // per ETH. Publishing the prior reading as fresh would be unsafe, so invalidate instead.
 193:             priceAverage = 0;
 194:             lastUpdate = 0;
 195:             emit Invalidated(meanTick24);
 196:             return;
 197:         }
 198: 
 199:         priceAverage = price;
 200:         lastUpdate = block.timestamp;
 201:         emit Updated(meanTick24, price, timeElapsed);
 202:     }
 203: 
 204:     function _setBaseline(int56 cumulative, uint32 timestamp) private {
 205:         tickCumulativeLast = cumulative;
 206:         blockTimestampLast = timestamp;
 207:         if (!baselineInitialized) {
 208:             baselineInitialized = true;
 209:             emit BaselineInitialized(cumulative, timestamp);
 210:         }
 211:     }
 212: 
 213:     /// @dev v4 tick price is currency1/currency0 in raw units. Here that is OMR-wei/ETH-wei.
 214:     ///      `mulDiv` supplies the 512-bit intermediate for sqrtPriceX96^2.
 215:     function _omrPerEthAtTick(int24 tick) private pure returns (uint256) {
 216:         uint256 sqrtPriceX96 = uint256(TickMath.getSqrtPriceAtTick(tick));
 217:         return Math.mulDiv(sqrtPriceX96, sqrtPriceX96 * 1e18, uint256(1) << 192);
 218:     }
 219: }
===== src/GenesisProceedsSplitter.sol 1-150 =====
   1: // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
   2: pragma solidity 0.8.26;
   3: 
   4: import {IERC20} from "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/IERC20.sol";
   5: import {SafeERC20} from "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/utils/SafeERC20.sol";
   6: import {ReentrancyGuard} from "@openzeppelin/contracts/utils/ReentrancyGuard.sol";
   7: import {IPoolManager} from "v4-core/interfaces/IPoolManager.sol";
   8: import {PoolId} from "v4-core/types/PoolId.sol";
   9: import {StateLibrary} from "v4-core/libraries/StateLibrary.sol";
  10: 
  11: /// @title GenesisProceedsSplitter
  12: /// @notice Ownerless recipient for the currency and token dust returned by Uniswap's LBPStrategy.
  13: ///
  14: /// @dev LBPStrategy uses one `recipient` for two materially different outcomes:
  15: ///        1. after a successful migration, residual currency and unused LP-token dust; and
  16: ///        2. after a failed migration, all recovered currency and the complete LP-token reserve.
  17: ///
  18: ///      Treating (2) as revenue would pay out a launch that never established its market. This
  19: ///      contract distinguishes the outcomes by reading the committed pool directly. OmertaHook
  20: ///      permits only the configured LBPStrategy to initialize that pool, and a failed inner
  21: ///      migration reverts the initialization with it, so a nonzero pool price is the authoritative
  22: ///      success signal.
  23: ///
  24: ///      Successful residual ETH is split 40% treasury / 36% Vig / 24% founder. Since the launcher
  25: ///      allocated 37.5% of raised ETH to LP first, those residual shares reproduce the original
  26: ///      whole-raise allocation: 25% treasury / 22.5% Vig / 15% founder. ERC-20 dust always returns
  27: ///      to treasury; on failure, both recovered ETH and the reserved OMR return there as well.
  28: contract GenesisProceedsSplitter is ReentrancyGuard {
  29:     using SafeERC20 for IERC20;
  30:     using StateLibrary for IPoolManager;
  31: 
  32:     uint256 public constant BPS = 10_000;
  33:     uint256 public constant TREASURY_BPS = 4_000;
  34:     uint256 public constant VIG_BPS = 3_600;
  35:     // Founder receives the exact remainder (2,400 bps), so rounding never strands wei.
  36: 
  37:     IPoolManager public immutable poolManager;
  38:     PoolId public immutable canonicalPoolId;
  39:     address payable public immutable treasuryRecipient;
  40:     address payable public immutable vigRecipient;
  41:     address payable public immutable founderRecipient;
  42: 
  43:     event ResidualDistributed(uint256 total, uint256 treasury, uint256 vig, uint256 founder);
  44:     event FailedLaunchRecovered(uint256 amount, address indexed treasury);
  45:     event TokenRecovered(address indexed token, uint256 amount, address indexed treasury);
  46: 
  47:     error ZeroAddress();
  48:     error NothingToDistribute();
  49:     error PoolNotInitialized();
  50:     error PoolAlreadyInitialized();
  51:     error TransferFailed(address recipient, uint256 amount);
  52: 
  53:     constructor(
  54:         IPoolManager poolManager_,
  55:         PoolId canonicalPoolId_,
  56:         address payable treasuryRecipient_,
  57:         address payable vigRecipient_,
  58:         address payable founderRecipient_
  59:     ) {
  60:         if (
  61:             address(poolManager_) == address(0) || treasuryRecipient_ == address(0) || vigRecipient_ == address(0)
  62:                 || founderRecipient_ == address(0)
  63:         ) revert ZeroAddress();
  64:         poolManager = poolManager_;
  65:         canonicalPoolId = canonicalPoolId_;
  66:         treasuryRecipient = treasuryRecipient_;
  67:         vigRecipient = vigRecipient_;
  68:         founderRecipient = founderRecipient_;
  69:     }
  70: 
  71:     receive() external payable {}
  72: 
  73:     /// @notice True only after the committed v4 pool has been initialized successfully.
  74:     function canonicalPoolInitialized() public view returns (bool) {
  75:         (uint160 sqrtPriceX96,,,) = poolManager.getSlot0(canonicalPoolId);
  76:         return sqrtPriceX96 != 0;
  77:     }
  78: 
  79:     /// @notice Permissionlessly split all residual native currency after successful migration.
  80:     function distributeResidual() external nonReentrant {
  81:         if (!canonicalPoolInitialized()) revert PoolNotInitialized();
  82:         uint256 total = address(this).balance;
  83:         if (total == 0) revert NothingToDistribute();
  84: 
  85:         uint256 treasury = (total * TREASURY_BPS) / BPS;
  86:         uint256 vig = (total * VIG_BPS) / BPS;
  87:         uint256 founder = total - treasury - vig;
  88: 
  89:         _send(treasuryRecipient, treasury);
  90:         _send(vigRecipient, vig);
  91:         _send(founderRecipient, founder);
  92:         emit ResidualDistributed(total, treasury, vig, founder);
  93:     }
  94: 
  95:     /// @notice Return all native currency to treasury while migration has not succeeded.
  96:     /// @dev Permissionless and non-redirectable. This is also safe before launch if ETH is sent here
  97:     ///      accidentally. Once the canonical pool exists, the residual split is the only path.
  98:     function recoverFailedLaunch() external nonReentrant {
  99:         if (canonicalPoolInitialized()) revert PoolAlreadyInitialized();
 100:         uint256 amount = address(this).balance;
 101:         if (amount == 0) revert NothingToDistribute();
 102:         _send(treasuryRecipient, amount);
 103:         emit FailedLaunchRecovered(amount, treasuryRecipient);
 104:     }
 105: 
 106:     /// @notice Return any ERC-20 dust or failed-migration token reserve to treasury.
 107:     /// @dev The launch currency is native ETH. Tokens are never part of the revenue split.
 108:     function recoverToken(IERC20 token) external nonReentrant {
 109:         if (address(token) == address(0)) revert ZeroAddress();
 110:         uint256 amount = token.balanceOf(address(this));
 111:         if (amount == 0) revert NothingToDistribute();
 112:         token.safeTransfer(treasuryRecipient, amount);
 113:         emit TokenRecovered(address(token), amount, treasuryRecipient);
 114:     }
 115: 
 116:     function _send(address payable recipient, uint256 amount) private {
 117:         if (amount == 0) return;
 118:         (bool ok,) = recipient.call{value: amount}("");
 119:         if (!ok) revert TransferFailed(recipient, amount);
 120:     }
 121: }

 168:     function buy(uint256 ballotDay, uint256 ethIn, uint256 minUnits, bytes calldata routeData)
 169:         external
 170:         onlyKeeper
 171:         nonReentrant
 172:         whenNotPaused
 173:         returns (address token, uint256 units)
 174:     {
 175:         if (adapter == address(0)) revert AdapterDisabled();
 176:         if (ethIn == 0 || minUnits == 0) revert ZeroAmount();
 177:         uint256 currentDay = block.timestamp / 1 days;
 178:         if (currentDay == 0 || ballotDay != currentDay - 1) revert UnexpectedBallotDay();
 179:         if (purchased[ballotDay]) revert AlreadyPurchased();
 180: 
 181:         (bytes32 assetKey, address resolvedToken) = _resolveActiveBallot(ballotDay);
 182:         uint256 enforcedMinUnits = _enforcedMinimum(resolvedToken, ethIn, minUnits);
 183:         if (address(this).balance < ethIn) revert InsufficientBalance();
 184: 
 185:         // Effects before interaction. Any adapter/output failure reverts these writes atomically.
 186:         purchased[ballotDay] = true;
 187:         _chargeDailyCap(ethIn);
 188: 
 189:         token = resolvedToken;
 190:         units = _acquire(resolvedToken, ethIn, enforcedMinUnits, routeData);
 191:         emit StockBought(ballotDay, assetKey, token, ethIn, units, stockVault);
 192:     }

 194:     function _resolveActiveBallot(uint256 ballotDay) private view returns (bytes32 assetKey, address token) {
 195:         bool active;
 196:         (assetKey, token,,, active) = registry.resolveBallot(ballotDay);
 197:         if (!active || token == address(0)) revert BallotAssetInactive();
 198:     }

 200:     function _chargeDailyCap(uint256 ethIn) private {
 201:         uint256 currentDay = block.timestamp / 1 days;
 202:         uint256 newSpend = spentOnDay[currentDay] + ethIn;
 203:         if (newSpend > dailyEthCap) revert DailyCapExceeded();
 204:         spentOnDay[currentDay] = newSpend;
 205:     }
 206: 
 207:     function _enforcedMinimum(address token, uint256 ethIn, uint256 keeperMinUnits) private view returns (uint256) {
 208:         if (quoteOracle == address(0) || maxQuoteAge == 0) revert QuoteOracleDisabled();
 209:         (uint256 oracleMinUnits, uint256 observedAt) = IStockQuoteOracle(quoteOracle).minUnitsOut(token, ethIn);
 210:         if (oracleMinUnits == 0 || observedAt == 0 || observedAt > block.timestamp) revert QuoteUnavailable();
 211:         if (block.timestamp - observedAt > maxQuoteAge) revert QuoteStale();
 212:         return oracleMinUnits > keeperMinUnits ? oracleMinUnits : keeperMinUnits;
 213:     }
 214: 
 215:     function _acquire(address token, uint256 ethIn, uint256 minUnits, bytes calldata routeData)
 216:         private
 217:         returns (uint256 units)
 218:     {
 219:         uint256 beforeBalance = IERC20(token).balanceOf(stockVault);
 220:         IStockSwapAdapter(adapter).buy{value: ethIn}(token, stockVault, minUnits, routeData);
 221:         uint256 afterBalance = IERC20(token).balanceOf(stockVault);
 222:         if (afterBalance < beforeBalance || afterBalance - beforeBalance < minUnits) revert InsufficientOutput();
 223:         return afterBalance - beforeBalance;
 224:     }

 255:     /// @notice The most debt (18dp) this user may owe against their current collateral.

 257:         return (collateralOf(user) * scale * ltvBps) / BPS;

 260:     // �� deposit / withdraw �����������������������������������������������������������������������
 261: 
 262:     /// @notice Deposit collateral with a caller-chosen ERC-4626 share floor.
 263:     /// @dev    The floor is mandatory because a vault donation can move its exchange rate between
 264:     ///         quote construction and execution. Measuring the escrow's actual share-balance delta
 265:     ///         also prevents a vault from satisfying this check with a dishonest return value.
 266:     function deposit(uint256 assets, uint256 minSharesOut) external nonReentrant onlyAllowedCaller {
 267:         if (assets == 0 || minSharesOut == 0) revert ZeroAmount();
 268:         CollateralEscrow e = escrowOf[msg.sender];
 269:         if (address(e) == address(0)) {
 270:             e = new CollateralEscrow(msg.sender, asset, vault);
 271:             escrowOf[msg.sender] = e;
 272:             emit EscrowCreated(msg.sender, address(e));
 273:         }
 274:         // FlashGuard L1: this stamps the block, so a borrow or withdrawal in the SAME transaction
 275:         // reverts. That is what makes the atomic depositborrowexit round trip impossible.
 276:         _recordEntry(msg.sender);
 277: 
 278:         uint256 assetBalanceBefore = asset.balanceOf(address(e));
 279:         asset.safeTransferFrom(msg.sender, address(e), assets);
 280:         uint256 assetBalanceAfter = asset.balanceOf(address(e));
 281:         if (assetBalanceAfter < assetBalanceBefore) revert AssetTransferMismatch(0, assets);
 282:         uint256 received = assetBalanceAfter - assetBalanceBefore;
 283:         if (received != assets) revert AssetTransferMismatch(received, assets);
 284: 
 285:         uint256 shares = e.deployToVault(assets);
 286:         if (shares < minSharesOut) revert InsufficientShares(shares, minSharesOut);
 287:         principalOf[msg.sender] += assets;
 288:         emit Deposited(msg.sender, assets);
 289:     }
 290: 
 291:     function withdraw(uint256 assets) external nonReentrant onlyAllowedCaller notSameBlockAsEntry(msg.sender) {
 292:         if (assets == 0) revert ZeroAmount();
 293:         CollateralEscrow e = escrowOf[msg.sender];
 294:         if (address(e) == address(0)) revert NoEscrow();
 295: 
 296:         e.withdraw(assets, msg.sender);
 297:         // principal is a floor-tracked figure: withdrawing more than principal means the excess
 298:         // came from yield, and principal simply bottoms out at zero.
 299:         uint256 p = principalOf[msg.sender];
 300:         principalOf[msg.sender] = assets >= p ? 0 : p - assets;
 301: 
 302:         // the invariant, checked AFTER the move
 303:         if (debtOf[msg.sender] > maxDebtOf(msg.sender)) revert Undercollateralised();
 304:         emit Withdrawn(msg.sender, assets);
 305:     }
 306: 
 307:     // �� borrow �����������������������������������������������������������������������������������

 309:     /// @notice Draw `debtAmount` of DNR (18dp) against your escrow.
 310:     function mint(uint256 debtAmount) external nonReentrant onlyAllowedCaller notSameBlockAsEntry(msg.sender) {
 311:         if (debtAmount == 0) revert ZeroAmount();
 312:         if (address(escrowOf[msg.sender]) == address(0)) revert NoEscrow();
 313:         // 2.4's ordering: the protocol stops ISSUING before it stops paying. A thin buffer halts
 314:         // new debt here; it never touches the Transmuter's ability to honour existing claims.
 315:         if (!transmuter.bufferHealthy()) revert BufferUnhealthy();
 316: 
 317:         _meter(_mintFlow, debtAmount, mintPerBlockCap, mintPerDayCap);
 318: 
 319:         uint256 newDebt = debtOf[msg.sender] + debtAmount;
 320:         if (newDebt > maxDebtOf(msg.sender)) revert Undercollateralised();
 321:         debtOf[msg.sender] = newDebt;
 322: 
 323:         debtToken.mint(msg.sender, debtAmount);
 324:         // `bufferHealthy` depends on total DNR supply. The pre-check above protects an already-thin
 325:         // reserve; this post-state check prevents the issuance itself from crossing the floor.
 326:         // Reverting here atomically rolls back both the token mint and the debt write.
 327:         if (!transmuter.bufferHealthy()) revert BufferUnhealthy();
 328:         emit Minted(msg.sender, debtAmount);
 329:     }
 330: 
 331:     // �� repay ������������������������������������������������������������������������������������
 332: 
 333:     /// @notice Repay in UNDERLYING. The assets go to the Transmuter as backing.
 334:     /// @dev    Repayment is deliberately not "burn your DNR here": the Transmuter is the single
 335:     ///         burn authority (Denari's header), and paying in underlying is strictly better for the
 336:     ///         system - it clears debt AND deepens the buffer, leaving the outstanding DNR fully
 337:     ///         redeemable rather than merely destroyed. Anyone holding DNR who wants out uses the
 338:     ///         Transmuter directly.
 339:     ///
 340:     ///         Overpayment is REFUSED rather than banked as a credit. A negative debt balance is a
 341:     ///         claim on the protocol, and this batch issues none. Clamping at zero prevents a user
 342:     ///         acquiring repayment credit beyond the debt actually cleared.
 343:     function repay(uint256 assets) external nonReentrant {
 344:         if (assets == 0) revert ZeroAmount();
 345:         uint256 d = debtOf[msg.sender];
 346:         if (d == 0) revert ZeroAmount();
 347: 
 348:         uint256 asDebt = assets * scale;
 349:         if (asDebt > d) {
 350:             asDebt = d;
 351:             assets = (d + scale - 1) / scale; // round UP so the protocol is never short-changed
 352:         }
 353: 
 354:         asset.safeTransferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), assets);
 355:         debtOf[msg.sender] = d - asDebt;
 356: 
 357:         asset.forceApprove(address(transmuter), assets);
 358:         transmuter.fund(assets);
 359:         asset.forceApprove(address(transmuter), 0);
 360: 
 361:         emit Repaid(msg.sender, assets, asDebt);
 362:     }
 363: 
 364:     // �� harvest: the self-repaying half ����������������������������������������������������������
 365: 
 366:     /// @notice Realise the yield a user's escrow has earned above principal and apply it to debt.
 367:     /// @dev    Permissionless by design - anyone may harvest anyone. It can only ever REDUCE the
 368:     ///         target's debt and move their yield into backing, so there is nothing for a caller to
 369:     ///         extract and no reason to gate it. That also means a keeper can run it, which is what
 370:     ///         makes the loan self-repaying without the user doing anything.
 371:     function harvest(address user) external nonReentrant {
 372:         CollateralEscrow e = escrowOf[user];
 373:         if (address(e) == address(0)) revert NoEscrow();
 374: 
 375:         uint256 total = e.totalAssets();
 376:         uint256 p = principalOf[user];
 377:         if (total <= p) revert NothingToHarvest();
 378:         uint256 yield_ = total - p;
 379: 
 380:         uint256 d = debtOf[user];
 381:         if (d == 0) revert NothingToHarvest(); // leave the yield compounding in the escrow
 382: 
 383:         // THE PERFORMANCE FEE comes off the yield BEFORE it services debt, so `net` is what this
 384:         // harvest can actually repay. A zero recipient disables it (fail-safe: an unset recipient
 385:         // under-charges rather than burning the yield).
 386:         uint16 feeBps = feeRecipient == address(0) ? 0 : harvestFeeBps;
 387:         uint256 net = yield_ - (yield_ * feeBps) / BPS;
 388: 
 389:         // Take the whole (net) yield unless it would clear more than is owed, in which case take
 390:         // only what the debt needs - rounded UP, so the assets moved always cover the debt cleared
 391:         // and never the other way round.
 392:         uint256 take = net;
 393:         uint256 asDebt = take * scale;
 394:         if (asDebt > d) {
 395:             take = (d + scale - 1) / scale; // ceil(d / scale)
 396:             if (take > net) take = net;
 397:             asDebt = take * scale;
 398:             if (asDebt > d) asDebt = d;
 399:         }
 400: 
 401:         // The fee is PROPORTIONAL TO WHAT WAS TAKEN, not to the escrow balance: fee / (take + fee)
 402:         // == feeBps / BPS. When the debt is smaller than the yield, the untouched remainder keeps
 403:         // compounding for the user and is never billed - which is what stops this becoming a
 404:         // management fee that charges for the same money every harvest.
 405:         //   In exact arithmetic both branches satisfy `take + fee <= yield_`:
 406:         //     debt-bound   take = need <= net = yield_(1-f)  =>  take + fee = need/(1-f) <= yield_
 407:         //     yield-bound  take = net  = yield_(1-f)         =>  take + fee = yield_ exactly
 408:         //
 409:         // THE CLAMP BELOW IS REACHABLE, and the argument above is why it is easy to believe it is
 410:         // not. `net` is computed with a floored fee, so net >= yield_(1-f) - it can sit up to one
===== src/Transmuter.sol 130-220 =====
 130:     }
 131: 
 132:     // �� the buffer �������������������������������������������������������������������������������
 133: 
 134:     /// @notice Underlying required, in asset units, to consider the buffer healthy.
 135:     function requiredBuffer() public view returns (uint256) {
 136:         return (debtToken.totalSupply() * bufferFloorBps) / BPS / scale;
 137:     }
 138: 
 139:     /// @notice False halts ISSUANCE in the Alchemist. It never halts redemption - see the header.
 140:     function bufferHealthy() external view returns (bool) {
 141:         return reserves >= requiredBuffer();
 142:     }
 143: 
 144:     /// @notice Add underlying backing. Called by the Alchemist on repay/harvest.
 145:     function fund(uint256 assets) external nonReentrant {
 146:         if (!funder[msg.sender]) revert NotFunder();
 147:         if (assets == 0) revert ZeroAmount();
 148:         uint256 balanceBefore = asset.balanceOf(address(this));
 149:         asset.safeTransferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), assets);
 150:         uint256 balanceAfter = asset.balanceOf(address(this));
 151:         if (balanceAfter < balanceBefore) revert AssetTransferMismatch(0, assets);
 152:         uint256 received = balanceAfter - balanceBefore;
 153:         if (received != assets) revert AssetTransferMismatch(received, assets);
 154:         reserves += received;
 155:         emit Funded(msg.sender, assets);
 156:     }
 157: 
 158:     // �� redemption �������������������������������������������������������������������������������
 159: 
 160:     /// @notice Burn `debtAmount` DNR (18dp) for the same value of underlying (asset dp), 1:1.
 161:     /// @dev    Checks-effects-interactions: reserves fall and the burn happens before the transfer
 162:     ///         out, and `nonReentrant` backstops it. The burn is of THIS contract's own balance
 163:     ///         after pulling the tokens in - see Denari.burn's note on why that matters.
 164:     /// @dev    **No `onlyAllowedCaller` here, and that absence is deliberate.** An earlier cut of this
 165:     ///         contract carried the L2 allowlist on this function, which contradicted its own header:
 166:     ///         most redemption arbitrage is executed BY CONTRACTS, so an allowlist on the redeem path
 167:     ///         would block the peg defense while claiming to protect it. The flow caps below bound a
 168:     ///         drain without gating who may repair the peg - that is the right tool for this path.
 169:     function redeem(uint256 debtAmount) external nonReentrant returns (uint256 assetsOut) {
 170:         if (debtAmount == 0) revert ZeroAmount();
 171:         // Round DOWN in the protocol's favour: a redeemer can never extract more underlying than
 172:         // the debt they burned represents. With scale == 1 this is exact.
 173:         assetsOut = debtAmount / scale;
 174:         if (assetsOut == 0) revert ZeroAmount(); // dust below one asset unit would burn for nothing
 175:         if (assetsOut > reserves) revert InsufficientReserves();
 176: 
 177:         _meter(_redeemFlow, assetsOut, redeemPerBlockCap, redeemPerDayCap);
 178: 
 179:         // effects
 180:         reserves -= assetsOut;
 181: 
 182:         // pull the debt in, then burn our own balance (never a third party's - no allowance is
 183:         // checked inside Denari.burn, and this is the reason that is safe)
 184:         IERC20(address(debtToken)).safeTransferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), debtAmount);
 185:         debtToken.burn(address(this), debtAmount);
 186: 
 187:         asset.safeTransfer(msg.sender, assetsOut);
 188:         emit Redeemed(msg.sender, debtAmount, assetsOut);
 189:     }
 190: }

  17: /// @title OmrV4TwapOracle - a geometric TWAP for the canonical native ETH/OMR Uniswap v4 pool.
  18: ///
  19: /// @notice Feeds the unchanged `IOmrOracle` surface consumed by OmertaBond. Uniswap v4's
  20: ///         PoolManager exposes current slot0 state but does not maintain the v2/v3 historical
  21: ///         observation series the bond mint wall needs. The canonical OmertaHook therefore keeps a
  22: ///         minimal tick-time accumulator on every successful swap; this contract closes bounded
  23: ///         windows over that cumulative and converts the arithmetic mean tick into OMR per ETH.
  24: ///
  25: ///         MISSED POKES DO NOT LOSE THE PRICE PATH. Keeper calls only decide when a completed window
  26: ///         is published. Every swap updates the source accumulator, and quiet time is brought forward
  27: ///         counterfactually at read time. A keeper outage makes the oracle stale; it cannot make a
  28: ///         spot price masquerade as a TWAP.
  29: ///
  30: ///         DEPLOYMENT DOES NOT REQUIRE AN OPEN POOL. The immutable source and pool identity may be
  31: ///         pinned before genesis migration. Until an initialized observation seeds a baseline,
  32: ///         consult returns no quote. The first seed also returns no quote: only a full subsequent
  33: ///         PERIOD of initialized pool history can produce the first reading.
  34: ///
  35: ///         BOTH SIDES OF THE WINDOW ARE BOUNDED. A call before `PERIOD` reverts (or no-ops when it
  36: ///         arrives through the hook's observer seam). A call after `PERIOD * MAX_WINDOW_MULT`
  37: ///         discards the obsolete interval, clears the public reading, and re-baselines. Recovery is
  38: ///         one honest window. This is the same fail-closed policy as OmrTwapOracle's audited V2 path.
  39: ///
  40: ///         The result is a geometric time-weighted mean, the standard tick-cumulative construction:
  41: ///         ticks are logarithms of price, so averaging tick and converting once yields geometric
  42: ///         rather than arithmetic mean price. OMR and native ETH both use 18 decimals, making the
  43: ///         v4 currency1/currency0 raw-unit ratio exactly OMR-wei per ETH-wei.
  44: contract OmrV4TwapOracle is IOmrOracle, IOmrHookObserver {

  48:     uint32 public constant MIN_PERIOD = 10 minutes;
  49:     uint32 public constant MAX_WINDOW_MULT = 4;
  50:

  84:     /// @param source_ the exact OmertaHook used by the canonical v4 pool
  85:     /// @param omr_ OMR; native ETH is fixed as currency0 and OMR as currency1
  86:     /// @param fee_ the canonical pool fee committed by the launch configuration
  87:     /// @param tickSpacing_ the canonical pool tick spacing committed by the launch configuration
  88:     /// @param period_ minimum number of chain seconds in one published observation window
  89:     constructor(IOmrV4ObservationSource source_, address omr_, uint24 fee_, int24 tickSpacing_, uint32 period_) {
  90:         if (address(source_) == address(0) || omr_ == address(0)) revert ZeroAddress();
  91:         if (address(source_).code.length == 0) revert ContractRequired(address(source_));
  92:         if (omr_.code.length == 0) revert ContractRequired(omr_);

  94:         if (!IERC165(address(source_)).supportsInterface(type(IOmrV4ObservationSource).interfaceId)) {

  97:

 125:     /// @notice Permissionless keeper entry point. The first initialized call seeds a baseline;
 126:     ///         nobody can close a window before a full PERIOD after that baseline.
 127:     function update() external {

 131:     /// @inheritdoc IOmrHookObserver
 132:     /// @dev Called through OmertaHook.pokeObserver after PoolManager settlement. Frequent swap
 133:     ///      notifications simply no-op until a full window is available, avoiding a revert on every
 134:     ///      trade while preserving the strict behavior of the direct keeper entry point.
 135:     function observe(PoolKey calldata key) external {

 146:     function _update(bool revertIfEarly) private {
 147:         (int56 cumulative, uint32 timestamp, bool initialized) = source.currentTickCumulative(poolId);
 148:         if (!initialized) {
 149:             if (revertIfEarly) revert PoolNotInitialized();
 150:             return;
 151:         }
 152: 
 153:         if (!baselineInitialized) {
 154:             _setBaseline(cumulative, timestamp);
 155:             return;
 156:         }
 157: 
 158:         uint32 timeElapsed;
 159:         unchecked {
 160:             timeElapsed = timestamp - blockTimestampLast;
 161:         }
 162:         if (timeElapsed < PERIOD) {
 163:             if (revertIfEarly) revert PeriodNotElapsed(timeElapsed, PERIOD);
 164:             return;
 165:         }
 166: 
 167:         if (timeElapsed > PERIOD * MAX_WINDOW_MULT) {
 168:             _setBaseline(cumulative, timestamp);
 169:             arithmeticMeanTick = 0;
 170:             priceAverage = 0;
 171:             lastUpdate = 0;
 172:             emit Rebaselined(timeElapsed);
 173:             return;
 174:         }
 175: 
 176:         int56 cumulativeDelta;
 177:         unchecked {
 178:             cumulativeDelta = cumulative - tickCumulativeLast;
 179:         }
 180:         int56 denominator = int56(uint56(timeElapsed));
 181:         int56 meanTick = cumulativeDelta / denominator;
 182:         // Solidity rounds signed division toward zero. Tick TWAPs conventionally round toward
 183:         // negative infinity so a negative fractional tick does not become a more favorable price.
 184:         if (cumulativeDelta < 0 && (cumulativeDelta % denominator != 0)) meanTick--;
 185: 
 186:         int24 meanTick24 = int24(meanTick);
 187:         uint256 price = _omrPerEthAtTick(meanTick24);
 188:         _setBaseline(cumulative, timestamp);
 189:         arithmeticMeanTick = meanTick24;
 190:         if (price == 0) {
 191:             // At an extreme negative tick the 18-decimal quote genuinely rounds below one OMR wei
 192:             // per ETH. Publishing the prior reading as fresh would be unsafe, so invalidate instead.
 193:             priceAverage = 0;
 194:             lastUpdate = 0;
 195:             emit Invalidated(meanTick24);
 196:             return;
 197:         }
 198: 
 199:         priceAverage = price;
 200:         lastUpdate = block.timestamp;
 201:         emit Updated(meanTick24, price, timeElapsed);
 202:     }
 203: 
 204:     function _setBaseline(int56 cumulative, uint32 timestamp) private {
 205:         tickCumulativeLast = cumulative;
 206:         blockTimestampLast = timestamp;
 207:         if (!baselineInitialized) {
 208:             baselineInitialized = true;
 209:             emit BaselineInitialized(cumulative, timestamp);
 210:         }
 211:     }

 213:     /// @dev v4 tick price is currency1/currency0 in raw units. Here that is OMR-wei/ETH-wei.
 214:     ///      `mulDiv` supplies the 512-bit intermediate for sqrtPriceX96^2.
 215:     function _omrPerEthAtTick(int24 tick) private pure returns (uint256) {
 216:         uint256 sqrtPriceX96 = uint256(TickMath.getSqrtPriceAtTick(tick));
 217:         return Math.mulDiv(sqrtPriceX96, sqrtPriceX96 * 1e18, uint256(1) << 192);
 218:     }

codex
Manual review has confirmed one live conditional issue in the legacy v4 oracle path: an initialized pool with zero active liquidity can move price at zero token cost and later publish that interval as fresh. The executable regression demonstrates the full trace. Market V2’s state layer explicitly rejects zero-liquidity epochs, so this finding does not automatically transfer to the current Market V2 oracle path. No critical/high current-source exploit is confirmed so far.

# Defensive red-team report

## Scope

Reviewed repository HEAD `e56cf576065c5f1bbb9bb55115f5961267f7d654`, including the dirty working tree:

- 97 Solidity files under `omerta-contracts/src`
- 40 core/acquisition files
- 12 Market V2 files
- 9 interfaces
- 1 guarded-auction wrapper
- 35 vendored auction/dependency files
- 43 untracked Solidity source files, including the player-genesis stack

Methods followed the pinned Pashov, Plamen, and Trail of Bits revisions required by [SECURITY-REVIEW-POLICY.md](C:/Users/Jorge/Documents/Omerta/omerta-contracts/SECURITY-REVIEW-POLICY.md).

## Verified current finding

### M-02 — Medium, conditional oracle manipulation

An initialized legacy v4 pool with zero active liquidity permits a swap that changes the tick without transferring either asset. The resulting interval can later be published as a fresh TWAP.

Trace:

1. [`beforeInitialize`](C:/Users/Jorge/Documents/Omerta/omerta-contracts/src/OmertaHook.sol:439) authorizes pool initialization but does not require liquidity.
2. [`afterSwap`](C:/Users/Jorge/Documents/Omerta/omerta-contracts/src/OmertaHook.sol:532) records the post-swap tick even when both swap deltas are zero.
3. [`currentTickCumulative`](C:/Users/Jorge/Documents/Omerta/omerta-contracts/src/OmertaHook.sol:555) accrues that tick over time without liquidity information.
4. [`OmrV4TwapOracle._update`](C:/Users/Jorge/Documents/Omerta/omerta-contracts/src/OmrV4TwapOracle.sol:146) publishes the derived price and current timestamp.
5. The executable proof at [ComprehensiveMarketAudit.t.sol:113](C:/Users/Jorge/Documents/Omerta/omerta-contracts/test/audit/ComprehensiveMarketAudit.t.sol:113) moves `sqrtPriceX96` from `Q96` to `2*Q96` at zero token/ETH cost and later publishes approximately `4e18` OMR/ETH.

Impact: any bond or other issuance path trusting this oracle while canonical liquidity is absent can accept an economically unsupported price.

Market V2 does not inherit this exact path: its market-state tests reject zero-liquidity epochs. The finding remains live for consumers of the legacy `OmrV4TwapOracle`.

## Verified configuration/liveness risks

- [GenesisProceedsSplitter.sol:80](C:/Users/Jorge/Documents/Omerta/omerta-contracts/src/GenesisProceedsSplitter.sol:80): one immutable recipient that permanently rejects ETH makes the atomic success distribution permanently unavailable. No attacker can redirect funds, but deployment configuration can strand them.
- [OmertaFees.sol:128](C:/Users/Jorge/Documents/Omerta/omerta-contracts/src/OmertaFees.sol:128): the contract sends 25% to Vig and 75% to one recipient. Treasury/community subdivisions described off-chain are not enforced on-chain.
- Player genesis requires every participant to be settled before migration. Settlement is permissionless, but large eligible-address sets create keeper/enumeration pressure before the deadline.
- The genesis configurator remains trusted to select semantically correct auction, hook, token, Permit2, PoolManager, and PositionManager implementations. Runtime-codehash pinning does not protect against upgradeable proxies retaining the same proxy runtime.
- Vendored `ReentrancyGuardTransient` requires Cancun/EIP-1153 support. Deployment on a non-supporting chain is not covered by successful local Cancun compilation.

## Unverified leads

- A very large Merkle eligibility set with many dust contributors might turn per-wallet settlement into a deadline-driven operational DoS. No profitable or unavoidable exploit was established because anyone can settle each wallet.
- Forced ETH sent to PositionManager before genesis migration affects its dust accounting. No theft or invariant break was established; the primary remaining concern is dependency-specific migration failure.
- Market V2 economics—stability deployment timing, recovery hysteresis, solver competition, and turf adjudication—remain susceptible to governance/configuration abuse by their designated privileged roles. No privilege-escalation path was found.
- Fee-on-transfer, rebasing, callback-bearing, or proxy-upgradeable production assets can violate assumptions made by several modules. Most constructors expect standard OMR/ERC-20 behavior; production token selection remains an external prerequisite.

## Coverage and evidence

Inspected source and tests across:

- OMR, staking, fees, vouchers, NFTs, stock custody, bonds, bank, Alchemist/Transmuter
- Acquisition authority, vault/core, registry, reconciliation, health and gas modules
- All Market V2 hook, state, stability, arbitrage, bonds, commitments, turf, settlement and funding modules
- Player sale, coordinator, guarded CCA and the complete vendored dependency closure
- Access control, accounting conservation, callbacks/reentrancy, replay domains, oracle freshness/liquidity, rounding, lifecycle recovery, privilege and economic manipulation

Retained exact-source evidence includes:

- Player genesis: 29 tests/properties passed with fixed seed
- Market V2: 124 tests passed with fixed seed
- Earlier tracked-source baseline: 927 passed, one subsequently corrected harness failure
- Existing Slither triage: 31 selected tracked-source analyses; raw diagnostics were dispositioned

A new full-suite run was attempted but collided with another Forge process writing the shared `out/` directory and failed with `Access is denied`. It is recorded as unavailable evidence, not a pass or contract failure. Fresh static analysis was also unavailable for the new vendored/untracked closure.

## Conclusion

No current unprivileged critical/high exploit was confirmed. M-02 remains a verified medium conditional issue for the legacy oracle path. The remaining items are leads or deployment/configuration dependencies, not proven public exploits. This review is bounded to the exact source above and does not claim the contracts are bug-free or authorize deployment.
